[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.2, F33.3, F43.11, F60.3, F06.31\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what’s been troubling you or what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I… I came in because I’ve been thinking it might be better if I just didn’t wake up. And sometimes it feels like… dying would be what I deserve, like punishment for what I’ve done to my family and my crew.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things that happened, even if they weren’t entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Like I poison whatever I touch, and… maybe I deserve whatever comes from that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about yourself and your situation?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a while. About a year and a couple months, I think. It started a few months after my husband got hurt. It got bad enough that I had to stop leading the flooring jobs. I couldn’t really… keep track of the crew or deal with clients or make the calls on site safely. My apprentice and my husband started stepping in before I messed something up or got someone hurt.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to enjoy, like your work or time with family, don’t interest you as much anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to paint, cook big meals, run before jobs, go to theater rehearsals. Now it all just feels like chores from somebody else’s life. If I miss it, I don’t really care.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about how you would end your life, or have you made any plans about it?\n\nPatient: I… I haven’t really made a plan. But I keep thinking it might be better if I just didn’t wake up. Sometimes it feels like dying would be… fair, I guess. Like punishment for what I’ve done to my family and my crew.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any specific plans about how you would end your life?\n\nPatient: No… no specific plan. It’s more that I came in because I keep thinking I’d be better off not waking up. Sometimes it feels like dying would be… what I deserve, for what I’ve put my family and my crew through.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of time during this last year and a couple of months when you felt significantly better, even for a few weeks, where these feelings lifted or weren’t as strong?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… maybe some weeks weren’t as bad, like I could get through the day a little easier. But most of the time it’s still been there—the guilt, being tired, not caring about things… and those thoughts about not being here. It hasn’t really lifted.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. About a year and a couple months, I think. It started a few months after my husband got hurt. Since then it’s just kind of stayed with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way most days, and has it affected your daily life, like work, relationships, or things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Most days, yeah. It got bad enough that I had to stop leading the flooring jobs. I couldn’t really… keep track of the crew or deal with clients or make the calls on site safely. My apprentice and my husband started stepping in before I messed something up or got someone hurt.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to enjoy, like your work or time with family, don’t interest you as much anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to paint, cook big meals, run before jobs, go to theater rehearsals. Now it all just feels like chores from somebody else’s life. If I miss it, I don’t really care.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about how you would end your life, or have you made any plans about it?\n\nPatient: I… I haven’t really made a plan. But I keep thinking it might be better if I just didn’t wake up. Sometimes it feels like dying would be… fair, I guess. Like punishment for what I’ve done to my family and my crew.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any specific plans about how you would end your life?\n\nPatient: No… no specific plan. It’s more that I came in because I keep thinking I’d be better off not waking up. Sometimes it feels like dying would be… what I deserve, for what I’ve put my family and my crew through.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of time during this last year and a couple of months when you felt significantly better, even for a few weeks, where these feelings lifted or weren’t as strong?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… maybe some weeks weren’t as bad, like I could get through the day a little easier. But most of the time it’s still been there—the guilt, being tired, not caring about things… and those thoughts about not being here. It hasn’t really lifted.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of time during this last year and a couple of months when you felt significantly better, even for a few weeks, where these feelings lifted or weren’t as strong?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Maybe a week here or there felt a little less heavy, but… most of the time it’s still been there. The guilt, being worn out, not really wanting to do much… and the thoughts about death. It hasn’t really gone away.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down and having these thoughts, have you ever had any other times in your life when you felt this way?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure. I’ve had rough patches before, I guess, but I don’t know if it was like this. I don’t really want to get into all that right now.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down and having these thoughts, have you ever had any other times in your life when you felt similarly—down, guilty, or not interested in things—for a period of weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I’ve had times where I was down before, yeah, but… I can’t say if it was for weeks like this or the same kind of thing. I don’t really remember it clearly.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you recall if you ever had a period lasting weeks or more before this current one where you felt down, guilty, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I honestly can’t say. I know I’ve had low times before, but remembering if it lasted weeks or if it was like this… it’s kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down and having these thoughts, were there any times in your life when you felt similarly—down, guilty, or not interested in things—for weeks at a time, and were those times clearly separated by at least a couple of months where you felt better?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I can’t really separate it out like that. I’ve had times I felt low, but whether it was weeks and then a couple months better after… I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: At work I've caught myself pacing the same hallway with my trowel in my hand, checking the grout lines over and over, then freezing in a doorway because I felt like moving would make something bad happen.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: My face feels blank and I barely talk unless someone pushes me, and even with my husband and the crew I mostly sit there like I'm behind glass.\n-
